## Document 055: 2026年9月价格数据点评：如何看待PPI-CPI剪刀差走扩？

价格数据边际改善释放何种信号？，2026
年9 月9 日

如何理解价格数据的回落？，2026 年 8 月
9 日

PPI 环比转负，下半年物价表现或回归基
本面，2026 年7 月9 日

如何看待PPI-CPI 剪刀差走扩？ 
        ——2026 年9 月价格数据点评

事件：2026 年 9 月全国居民消费价格指数（CPI）同比 0.9%（前
值 0.8%），环比 0.2%（前值 0.4%），1-9 月 CPI 同比 0.9%（前值
0.9%）；核心 CPI 同比 1%（前值 1%），环比0（前值 0.1%），1-9 月
核心 CPI 同比 1.1%（前值 1.1%）；工业生产者出厂价格指数（PPI）
同比5%（前值3.8%），环比1.2%（前值0.4%），1-9 月PPI 同比2.3%
（前值2%）。 
受油价、芯片等涨价影响，CPI 环比连续两个月为正,9 月略高于
季节性水平，CPI 同比涨幅较上月小幅扩大；1-9 月，CPI 同比及核心
CPI 同比均较上年底 回升但仍低于上半年水平 ，价格持续修复 但修复
节奏放缓。9 月 CPI 同比增长 0.9%，较上月回升 0.1 个百分点，连续
两个月回升，环比延续正增长， 为 0.2%，强于季节性水平（2023 年-
2025 年，9 月 CPI 环比均值为 0.1%）。9 月核心 CPI（剔除食品和能
源）同比增长 1%，与上月持平，仍位于较低水平， 环比 0，持平季节
性水平（2023 年-2025 年，9 月核心CPI 环比均值为0）。1-9 月，CPI
同比及核心 CPI 同比分别为 0.9%和 1.1%，均与 1-8 月持平，较上年底
和上年同期水平回升，但均较上半年回落 0.1 个百分点，仍位于较低
区间水平，价格修复的基础有待进一步夯实。 
 油价仍是 CPI 环比维持正增的重要支撑， 同时受成本走高影响， 本
月移动电话机、计算机、数据存储设备和平板电脑 价格环比上涨、
对CPI 环比维持正增带来一定助力。工业消费品中，受国际市场价格
变动影响，9 月国内汽油价格环比上涨4.9%，连续两个月环比正增，
油价仍是CPI 环比维持正增的重要支撑。受芯片等成本上涨影响，从
7 月开始，多家手机厂商已经陆续上调部分在售产品价格。9 月 1 日，
www.ccxi.com.cn

www.ccxi.com.cn 
2026 年9 月价格数据点评  
如何看待PPI-CPI 剪刀差走扩？ 
华为、小米、荣耀多款手机官方调整售价，单次涨幅从 200 元至 1000 元不等。9 月移动电话
机、计算机、数据存储设备和平板电脑价格环比分别上涨 3.7%、2.0%、1.7%和 1.5%，合计影
响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.04 个百分点。此外，秋装上市，带动服装价格环比上涨 0.7%，影响 CPI
环比上涨约0.03 个百分点。 
 暑期效应消退，服务价格环比由涨转降，持平季节性水平，对 CPI 环比的影响由上月支撑转
为拖累，其中飞机票、宾馆住宿等 假期出行相关服务价格环比均由正转负。 9 月，服务价格
同比上涨0.8%，涨幅与上月相同，服务价格环比由上月上涨0.1%转为下降0.2%，持平季节性
（2023-2025 年9 月，服务价格环比均值为-0.2%），对CPI 环比的影响由上月上拉约0.04 个
百分点转为下拉约 0.08 个百分点。具体来看，随着暑假结束，飞机票、宾馆住宿环比均由正
转负，飞机票、宾馆住宿和旅行社收费价格环比分别下降 8.7%、7.0%和 6.0%，合计影响 CPI
环比下降约0.12 个百分点。 
 食品价格环比维持正增长 ，但低于季节性水平 ，同比降幅边际扩大，食品价格仍偏弱 。9 月
食品价格同比下降 1.7%，降幅比上月扩大 0.3 个百分点，影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.29 个百分
点，环比上涨0.4%，连续两个月正增长，影响CPI 环比上涨约0.06 个百分点，但仍低于季节
性水平（2023-2025 年 9 月，食品价格环比均值为 0.6%）。其中，受猪肉供给结构调整以及
暑假、中秋和国庆备货需求带动，鲜菜及猪肉价格环比连续 3 个月为正。此外，休渔期结束，
水产品价格环比连续两个月为负。

如何看待PPI-CPI 剪刀差走扩？ 
受输入性因素、 上游原材料行业供需关系调整 、人工智能行业快速发展提振相关行业需
求等带动，9 月PPI 环比连续两个月正增长，同比5%、升至2022 年7 月以来高点。1-9 月PPI
同比2.3%，较上半年上行0.8 个百分点，为2023 年以来的高点。9 月PPI 同比5%，较上月上
行 1.2 个百分点，PPI 环比连续两个月维持正增，9 月环比上涨 1.2%。PPI 分项中，生产资料
价格同比涨幅较上月扩大 1.5 个百分点，为 6.5%，是 PPI 同比上行的重要支撑，环比连续两
个月正增，为1.4%；生活资料价格同比降幅连续3 个月收窄，9 月较上月收窄0.4 个百分点，
为-0.1%，环比连续两个月正增，9 月为0.1%。 
 受中东局势与全球 AI 快速发展影响，国际油价、有色等大宗商品价格上涨带动国内相关行业
价格上涨，是 9 月 PPI 环比上行最重要的支撑；同时行业供需结构调整叠加季节性因素带动
煤炭、非金属矿物制品等部分行业价格上涨；人工智能快速发展，AI 产业链部分需求增加、
价格上涨，上述因素均对 PPI 形成支撑。从行业来看，受原油、有色金属等海外定价大宗商

2026 年9 月价格数据点评  
如何看待PPI-CPI 剪刀差走扩？ 
品价格上行影响，石油开采、精炼石油产品制造、有机化学原料制造价格环比分别上涨14.5%、
7.4%和5.4%，有色金属矿采选业、有色金属冶炼和压延加工业价格环比分别上涨1.6%和1.5%，
5 个行业合计影响PPI 环比上涨约0.63 个百分点，是本月PPI 环比改善最重要的支撑。同时，
受冬季供暖备煤需求增加、主产矿区供应阶段性偏紧 等因素影响，煤炭开采和洗选业、煤炭
加工价格环比分别上涨9.5%和13.6%。9 月是建筑施工旺季，钢材、水泥等建筑材料需求有所
回升，加之 煤炭等原材料成本上涨，非金属矿物制品业 （主要包括水泥、玻璃等） 价格环比
由负转正，上涨0.3%，黑色金属冶炼和压延加工业价格环比上涨0.2%，4 个行业合计影响PPI
环比上涨约 0.38 个百分点。此外，人工智能、算电协同等领域加快发展，电气机械和器材制
造业、计算机通信和其他电子设备制造业等 AI 产业链相关行业价格环比均上涨 0.4%，亦对
PPI 上行带来支撑。  
 生活资料价格环比连续两个月为正但仍位于低位区间，同比降幅收窄至 0 附近。生活资料价
格同比降幅连续 3 个月收窄但仍为负，9 月同比下降 0.1%，环比延续 8 月的正增长态势、连
续两个月为正，不过仍位于0 左右的低位区间，终端需求仍偏弱。

www.ccxi.com.cn 
2026 年9 月价格数据点评  
如何看待PPI-CPI 剪刀差走扩？

整体来看，今年以来物价整体呈现温和修复态势，呈现以下特征：一是上下游景气度存
在温差，中下游尤其下游中小企业仍在承受成本挤压 。今年以来，生产资料与生活资料同比
分化走势逐渐加大，二者剪刀差由年初 1 月的 0.4 个百分点扩大到 9 月的 6.6 个百分点。结
合 PMI 数据来看，截至 9 月，主要原材料购进价格分项与出厂价格剪刀差连续两个月扩大。
涨价能力和利润持续向上游资源行业集中，终端消费品价格整体仍偏弱，中下游制造业面临
成本挤压。二是 AI 产业链涨价正逐步成为影响物价走势的新增变量。从电气机械、算力设
施等上游环节，到存储器件、平板终端、手机等下游消费品，AI 相关产品价格表现均较好，
成为价格修复的重要力量。三是当前经济仍面临供给端涨价与需求端疲软并存的矛盾，PPI-
CPI 剪刀差扩大。自今年 4 月 PPI 同比首次超过 CPI 同比以来，PPI-CPI 正剪刀差由 4 月的
1.6%波动扩大至 9 月的 4.1%，9 月较 8 月扩大 1.1 个百分点。供需失衡的格局仍在延续，价
格修复仍受到掣肘。 
后续来看，9 月以来中东局势再度升级，国际原油价格波动上行，截至10 月13 日，WTI
原油期货结算价由 9 月底的 90.42 美元/桶，波动上行至 94.1 美元/桶。若油价持续高位运
行，短期石化产业链价格仍将位于较高水平， PPI 同比保持韧性仍有支撑，并沿产业链向
CPI 能源分项传导。但也要看到，当前生产端价格改善仍主要集中于能源、有色和部分新动
能行业，终端需求相关行业价格仍偏弱，核心CPI 始终在1%左右的较低水平徘徊，全面物价
上涨的可能性较低，完成2%的年度通胀目标难度仍较大。

## Document 060: 2026年9月经济数据点评：总量企稳的读数，结构分化的底色

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年9月经济数据点评：总量企稳的读数，结构分化的底色.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

请务必阅读正文后的重要声明部分  
1 
[Table_ReportInfo] 2026 年 10 月 19 日      
证 券研究报告 •宏观简评报告      
数据点评  
总量企稳的读数，结构分化的底色  
——2026 年 9 月经济数据点评

点评

西 南证券研究 院 
[Table_Summary]  摘要：GDP方面，2026年第三季度我国 GDP 同比增长 4.5%，较二季度回升
0.2 个百分点；前三季度增长 4.6%，仍处于全年 4.5%-5%的目标区间内。出
口和高技术制造业是主要支撑，全球 AI 产业景气带动电子产业链中游需求保
持强劲，但投资、消费恢复偏慢。预计四季度GDP 同比增长 4.5%-4.7%，出
口韧性与政策支持有望托底， 但地产调整和消费偏弱仍将制约经济增长。居民
收支和就业方面，前三季度居民人均可支配收入名义增长5.3%，增速较上半
年回升；人均消费支出名义增长 3.6%，消费增速未随收入同步加快。前三季
度城镇调查失业率平均为5.21%， 稳就业、促增收和职业技能培训政策有望改
善居民收入预期， 但消费倾向回升仍需观察。工业生产方面，前三季度规上工
业增加值同比增长 5.3%，9 月单月增长 5.5%，较 8 月提高 0.3 个百分点。高
技术产业和装备制造业增加值分别增长14.3%和 10.2%，AI 电子链、专用设
备及船舶航空等行业表现较强，但部分高景气产品增速放缓。预计全年规上工
业增加值增速为 5.2%-5.4%， 高技术制造业、 装备制造业及出口仍是主要支撑。
固定资产投资方面，前三季度固定资产投资同比下降 7.2%，扣除房地产开发
投资后下降 4.2%。高技术产业投资增长 4.9%。基建投资同比下降 4.2%，但
信息传输、 航空运输和水上运输业投资保持较快增长。剩余专项债、地方债结
存限额及新型政策性金融工具有望支撑基建和制造业投资。房地产投资方面，
前三季度房地产开发投资同比下降20.6%，9 月单月下降 27.19%，降幅继续
扩大。新开工、 竣工面积分别下降24.9%和 25.1%，商品房销售面积、销售额
分别下降 13.2%和 13.9%， 房企到位资金下降21.6%， 行业供需和资金面仍然
承压。9 月房价环比表现有所改善，购房贷款贴息政策也有望降低居民购房成
本。预计四季度销售降幅可能在贴息政策和低基数带动下收窄，但趋势性回暖
仍需观察。制造业投资方面， 前三季度制造业投资同比下降2.1%， 降幅较1-8
月收窄 0.2 个百分点；9 月单月下降 0.95%，降幅明显收窄，但部分改善可能
来自前期需求集中释放和低基数。铁路、船舶、航空航天及其他运输设备制造
业投资增长 15.8%，计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业增长9.6%。设备工
器具购置增速由一季度的 13.9%回落至前三季度的 9.7%，显示“两新”政策
拉动有所减弱。 预计四季度制造业投资单月增速在零附近波动，累计降幅有望
收窄至 1.5%-2.0%，行业分化或将延续。消费方面，前三季度社零同比增长
1.1%， 商品和服务零售总额增长2.6%， 其中服务零售额增长5.0%， 快于商品
零售的 1.0%。9 月单月社零增长 1.1%，较 8 月回升 0.7 个百分点，中秋错期
和低基数对增速形成提振。通讯器材类和烟酒类零售额分别增长 31.1%和
13.8%，而家电类增长 1.9%、汽车类下降 21.2%，汽车消费仍是主要拖累。
去年同期集中购车推高基数，今年补贴力度收缩、 部分需求提前释放，进一步
制约汽车消费。预计全年社零增速中枢约 1.4%；四季度低基数有望带动增速
小幅回升，但消费整体仍处于低位企稳阶段，趋势性复苏动力不足。 
 总量落在目标区间，出口走强与内需拖累并存。按不变价计算，2026 年第三
季度我国 GDP同比增长 4.5%，较二季度回升 0.2 个百分点，高于 Wind 一致
预期的 4.41%。2026 年前三季度 GDP 同比增长 4.6%，较上半年回落 0.1 个
百分点， 但仍高于Wind一致预期的 4.56%， 增速仍处于全年4.5%-5%的预期
目标区间内。 分产业看， 第三季度第一、 二、 三产业同比分别增长3.6%、3.4%

和 5.2%，其中第一产业增速回落 0.1 个百分点，二、三产业增速均回升。从
增长动力看，出口和高技术制造业是三季度经济增长的重要支撑。 受益于全球
AI 产业景气， 电子产业链中游产品需求保持强劲， 前三季度进出口表现超出市
场预期， 高技术产品出口的竞争优势持续巩固。与出口和制造业生产的较强韧
性相比，内需表现仍相对偏弱，投资和消费恢复偏慢，经济增长的内生动力有
待增强。三季度名义GDP 增速约为 6%，GDP 平减指数连续两个季度为正，
三季度初步核算为 1.33%，价格因素对名义经济增长的支撑有所增强。但价格
回升仍主要集中在上游能源和AI 相关行业，PPI回升的同时，CPI仍受居民消
费需求偏弱影响，整体处于低位。这表明上游涨价向下游商品和服务价格的传
导仍不充分，居民收入与消费之间也尚未形成明显的正向循环，价格回暖向内
需扩散仍需时间。预计出口韧性与政策支持仍是四季度 GDP的主要支撑。全
球 AI产业链景气有望带动四季度出口维持较高增速； 同时，5500亿元地方债

结存限额、PSL 利率下调至 1.5%并支持“六张网”建设，以及 8000亿元新
型政策性金融工具持续投放， 有望推动基建项目形成更多实物工作量， 并对制
造业投资形成支撑。 但以旧换新政策的拉动效应有所减弱， 房地产市场仍处于
深度调整阶段，内需恢复仍面临约束。整体来看，四季度经济仍将呈现外需支
撑较强、内需修复偏慢的格局。
 居民消费修复存在滞后性，就业景气保持平稳。从收入来看，2026 年前三季
度全国居民人均可支配收入同比名义增长 5.3%，扣除价格因素后实际增长
4.4%，增速较上半年均有所回升， 居民收入增长有所改善。从消费支出看，前
三季度全国居民人均消费支出同比名义增长3.6%，增速较上半年回落 0.1 个
百分点，实际增长 2.7%，与上半年持平，收入增速回升尚未同步转化为消费
增长， 居民消费修复节奏有所滞后。从消费结构看，前三季度食品烟酒、 居住、
医疗保健消费支出同比增速分别回升至4.47%、1.89%和 2.39%，其余消费支
出细项增速均较上半年边际回落， 反映消费支出的边际改善主要集中于部分日

常生活及刚性支出，其他消费领域的增长动能仍显不足，居民消费修复的广泛
性有待增强。就业方面，前三季度全国城镇调查失业率平均值为5.21%，较上
半年回升 0.01 个百分点，9 月全国城镇调查失业率为5.2%，环比下降 0.1 个
百分点， 短期就业状况有所改善， 但前三季度整体就业形势尚未出现明显变化。
10 月 10 日，国新办发布会相关负责人介绍， “十五五”时期将加大投资于人
力度，力争在此期间开展补贴性职业技能培训5000 万人次，新增取得技能证
书 4000 万人次、其中取得高级工以上技能证书1400 万人次。稳就业、促增
收与职业技能培训政策持续落地，居民就业稳定性及收入预期有望边际改善，
但考虑到前三季度收入增速回升而消费支出增速未同步加快， 居民消费倾向的
改善仍有待观察， 全年消费增长或延续温和修复态势。就业方面，预计城镇调
查失业率总体保持平稳， 后续就业政策对重点群体就业和劳动者技能提升的支
持力度有望进一步加大，但就业结构性矛盾的缓解仍需一定时间。
 单月工业生产边际改善，AI中间品强劲而消费电子承压。2026年前三季度，

全国规模以上工业增加值同比增长5.3%，增速与 1-8 月持平。9 月单月，工业
增加值同比增长 5.5%，较8 月提高 0.3 个百分点，符合 wind 一致预期 （5.5%）。
工业生产动能有所修复， 但整体增速尚未明显加快。前三季度高技术产业工业
增加值累计同比增长14.3%，较 1-8 月提升 0.1 个百分点；装备制造业工业增
加值累计同比增长 10.2%，均明显高于规上工业整体增速， 高技术相关产业仍
是工业增长的主要支撑。分三大门类看，9 月单月，制造业增加值同比增长
6.0%，较 8 月回落 0.1 个百分点； 电力、热力、燃气及水生产和供应业同比增
长 5.6%，采矿业同比由 8 月的下降转为上升 1.3%，增速均边际回升。三大门
类生产表现有所分化， 工业生产的边际改善尚未体现为各领域同步加速。从细
分行业看，9 月单月，计算机、 通信和其他电子设备制造业、专用设备制造业，
以及铁路、 船舶、 航空航天和其他运输设备制造业同比分别增长16.4%、12.1%、
11.7%，在主要行业中表现靠前。这反映 AI 电子链、 设备更新相关产业及船舶

航空等领域生产较为活跃，工业增长对高技术中间品、资本品及部分出口链条
的依赖度有所上升。石油和天然气开采业同比回升 3.6 个百分点至 9.8%，回
升幅度居前。通用设备制造业、电气机械及器材制造业、汽车制造业等制造业

9 月增速保持在 6%以上，但增速相比 8 月边际回落。分产品看，9 月单月，

 整体基建投资持续承压，新动能与新基建是结构性亮点。2026 年前三季度，

全国固定资产投资（不含农户）同比下降 7.2%，与 1-8 月增速持平。扣除房
地产开发的固定资产投资下降 4.2%，地产端仍是拖累整体投资的重要因素。
其中，知识产权产品投资同比增长9.3%。高技术产业投资同比增长4.9%，其
中信息服务业，航空、航天器及设备制造业， 电子及通信设备制造业投资分别
增长 20.2%、12.5%、7.6%，说明新质生产力、数字基础设施、航空水运等方
向仍有扩张。根据历史数据倒推测算，9 月单月， 固定资产投资同比下降9.69%，
降幅较 8 月单月收窄 3.82 个百分点，本轮边际修复或主要来自基建与制造业
端的积极变化。基建投资方面，统计局数据显示，前三季度基础设施投资同比
下降 4.2%，降幅相比 1-8 月扩大 0.2 个百分点。其中，信息传输业、航空运
输业、水上运输业投资分别增长 30.8%、13.8%、13.5%。根据历史数据测算，
前三季度广义基建投资同比下降 6.08%，降幅扩大 0.71 个百分点。9 月单月，
广义基建投资同比下降 10.48%，降幅收窄 4.3 个百分点。基建投资单月边际

改善可能来自低基数、天气扰动消退、专项债集中发行后项目资金到位。从资
金端看，截至 10 月 16 日，2026年超长期特别国债 1.3万亿元已经发行完毕，
与去年进度基本持平。9 月单月，地方政府新增专项债发行6204 亿元，同比
增速扩大至 50.18%，截至 9 月末发行进度 80.62%，略慢于去年同期。截至
10 月 16 日，今年地方政府专项债已发行 3.65 万亿元，年内剩余发行额度为
7546 亿元。在低基数、 天气扰动消退，以及剩余专项债、5500亿元地方债务
结存限额和 8000 亿元新型政策性金融工具加快落地形成实物工作量的支撑
下，年内投资单月降幅有望继续收窄。但资金到实物工作量有时滞、冬季施工
约束仍在，全年投资增速或将大幅收窄降幅、靠近零增长区间， 出现明显正增
的概率不大。
 地产市场仍处调整阶段，投资和销售继续走弱。2026 年前三季度，全国房地
产开发投资累计同比下降20.6%，降幅较 1-8 月扩大 0.7个百分点。根据历史
数据倒推测算，9 月单月，房地产开发投资同比下降27.19%，降幅相比 8 月

扩大 1.05 个百分点，说明房企开发活动仍然偏弱，房地产投资对整体投资的
拖累继续存在。供给端，相比于 1-8 月，房屋施工面积同比下降 12.8%，降幅
与上月持平；新开工面积和竣工面积同比降幅分别扩大至 24.9%和 25.1%；需
求端， 相比于 1-8 月，商品房销售面积和销售额降幅分别 扩大至 13.2%和
13.9%。资金端，前三季度房企到位资金同比下降 21.6%，降幅扩大 0.6个百
分点。其中国内贷款下降 33.1%，是各项来源中萎缩最快的一项，外部融资持
续承压、销售回款同步萎缩，从而制约房企资本开支。价格端，9 月单月，70
个大中城市中，一线城市商品住宅销售价格环比继续上涨， 二三线城市环比降
幅总体收窄，一二三线城市同比降幅均收窄，环比上涨城市个数比上月增加，
价格端的结构性改善与总量收缩并存。1-9 月二手房网签面积 6.17亿平方米，
同比增长 10.7%，已超过同期新建商品房销售面积5.65 亿平方米，需求载体
向二手房切换的特征进一步显现。政策方面，财政自 10 月 1 日起对符合条

件的新发放首套住房商业性个人住房贷款按 本金给予年化 1 个百分点、最
长 5 年的贴息。这是财政首次将贴息工具直接用于居民购房贷款，标志着
政策着力点正式由企业和投资端向居民终端需求延伸。 展望四季度， 购房贴
息叠加低基数效应， 有望推动商品房销售同比降幅收窄， 但销售趋势性回暖仍

需观察。 投资端受房企销售回款偏弱、 现房销售改革可能拉长资金回笼周期等

 制造业投资降幅收窄，结构性修复特征突出。2026 年前三季度，制造业投资
累计同比下降 2.1%，降幅较 1-8 月收窄 0.2 个百分点，增速在连续五个月边
际下降后边际回升。根据历史数据倒推测算，9 月单月，制造业投资同比下降
0.95%，降幅相比上月收窄5.11个百分点。9 月投资降幅收窄或受前期需求集
中释放和去年同期基数较低的影响。分行业看，13 个主要行业中，铁路、船
舶、航空航天和其他运输设备制造业投资增速最高，前三季度投资同比增速为
15.8%，计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业（+9.6%）、纺织业（+4.2%）增
速居前， 电气机械及器材制造业（+1.2%）、 通用设备制造业（+1.1%）同比亦
实现正增长，且增速相比1-8 月边际回升。但需要注意，中游装备制造业投资

的改善， 主要受到出口需求和设备更新政策的带动。 设备工器具购置增速已从
一季度的 13.9%回落至前三季度的 9.7%，说明“两新”政策的拉动作用正在
减弱。金属制品业（-10.1%） 、食品制造业（-10.3%）仍处于深度收缩区间，
是制造业投资的主要拖累因素，主要由于地产及消费链投资内生动能偏弱。预
计四季度制造业投资单月增速将在零附近波动，累计降幅有望进一步收窄至
1.5%-2.0%，但全年累计增速转正的难度仍较大。分行业看，航空航天、电子
设备等行业 有望继续支撑制造业投资，制造业投资的分化格局可能延续。
 高基数与中秋错期抬升读数，消费仍处于低位磨底阶段。2026 年前三季度，
全国社会消费品零售总额同比增长 1.1%，与 1-8 月增速持平，除汽车以外的
消费品零售额同比涨幅扩大 0.1 个百分点至 2.8%，说明汽车消费仍为消费增
速的拖累点。根据统计局披露， 前三季度社会消费商品和服务零售总额同比增
长 2.6%，其中服务零售额增长5.0%，增速相比 1-8 月加快 0.1 个百分点，商

品零售额增长 1.0%，增速持平 1-8 月表现。服务零售中，通讯信息服务类、
旅游咨询租赁服务类、文体休闲服务类零售额较快增长。9 月单月，社会消费
品零售总额同比涨幅扩大 0.7 个百分点至 1.1%，主要受中秋错期叠加低基数
影响。其中，9 月餐饮收入同比涨幅扩大 1 个百分点至 2.1%，商品零售同比
扩大 0.7 个百分点至 1%。从具体类别看，9 月单月主要类别商品零售同比增
速均边际回升。其中，通讯器材类、烟酒类同比分别增长 31.1%、13.8%，相
比 8 月增速分别回升 3.8、1.3 个百分点。通讯器材高增，主要由于手机新品在
9 月集中发布拉动换机需求释放，同时全年 2500 亿元以旧换新资金在 9 月全
部下达，换机需求提前兑现。家电和汽车是仅有的两类增速回落品类：家电类
零售额同比增长1.9%， 增速较8 月回落 0.4个百分点； 汽车类同比下降21.2%，
降幅扩大 2.7个百分点。汽车类消费收缩，主要由于去年四季度前多地以旧换
新补贴额度接近用尽，消费者集中购车，推动汽车单月销量创近年同期新高。

今年补贴力度有所收缩， 叠加去年集中购车对部分需求形成提前释放，汽车消
费增长因此承压。此外，石油及制品类、体育娱乐用品类、家具类、建筑及装
潢材料类、金银珠宝类等前期累计增速回落的品类，9 月单月增速边际回升幅
度均超 2 个百分点。该回暖主要依托中秋错期、低基数效应、价格因素与季节
性窗口。整体来看， 年内消费有望保持低位企稳， 趋势性回升的动力仍显不足。
四季度低基数效应有望带动社零增速小幅回升， 但汽车消费面临去年同期高基
数、以旧换新补贴力度减弱等压力，低基数带来的提振作用或较为有限。
 风险提示：政策落地节奏不及预期风险，海外经济超预期波动风险。已公布的
宏观或产业政策在实际执行过程中，可能因配套措施不完善、部门协调不畅、
地方执行力度差异或市场环境变化等因素，导致其推进速度、覆盖范围或最终
效果慢于或弱于市场预期，从而影响政策目标的实现与经济修复的进程； 主要
经济体的政治局势、经济数据或宏观政策出现意外变化，其幅度或频率超出市
场普遍预测。这种超预期波动可能引发全球金融市场动荡、 贸易条件恶化或外

需急剧收缩，并通过金融、贸易及预期渠道向国内传导，增加我国外部环境的
复杂性与不稳定性。

本报告署名分析师具有中国证券业协会授予的证券投资咨询执业资格并注册为证券分析师，报告所采用的数据均
来自合法合规渠道，分析逻辑基于分析师的职业理解，通过合理判断得出结论，独立、客观地出具本报告。分析师承
诺不曾因，不因，也将不会因本报告中的具体推荐意见或观点而直接或间接获取任何形式的补偿。

投资评级说明  
报告中投资建议所涉及的评级分为公司评级和行业评级（另有说明的除外） 。评级标准为报告发布日后 6 个月内
的相对市场表现，即：以报告发布日后 6 个月内公司股价（或行业指数）相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的涨跌幅
作为基准。其中：A 股市场以沪深 300 指数为基准， 新三板市场以三板成指 （针对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针
对做市转让标的）为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准；美国市场以纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数为基准。  
公司评级  
买入：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 20%以上  
持有：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 10%与 20%之间 
中性：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 -10%与 10%之间  
回避：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 -20%与-10%之间  
卖出：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 -20%以下  
行业评级  
强于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报高于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 5%以上 
跟随大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报介于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 -5%与 5%之间 
弱于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报低于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 -5%以下

### Page 1

ab 12 October 2026
Global Research
Genius Electronic Optical
Core resilience with CPO upside optionality
Raise PT to NT$1,250 on potential CPO contribution and intact core business
We reiterate Buy and raise our  price target to NT$1,250 from NT$700, rolling valuation
to 2028E and moving from P/B to a P/E-based SOTP that separately values the core lens
business and the emerging co-packaged optics (CPO) business. We believe Genius is
making progress sampling three precision components inside the fiber array unit (FAU)

targeting FAU integrators such as Coherent and FOCI.  We see potential for the company
to qualify the leading FAU module supplier into NVIDIA's CPO program. At the same
time, its core business is outperforming Largan, growing +1% YoY in Q326 vs. -11%
YoY for Largan as its share rises on periscope lens. Compared with the pressure on
Largan's margins from green electricity prices, we believe Genius should maintain its
margin, as its main production site is in Xiamen, whereas Largan's production in Taiwan

is affected by higher electricity prices.
Core intact: periscope share gains offset muted smartphone demand
September sales of NT$2.67bn (−2% MoM, +1% YoY) took Q326 to NT$7.82bn (+38%
QoQ, +1% YoY), in-line with VA consensus. Genius grew 10% YoY in 9M26 against 2%
at Largan, which we attribute to share gains in periscope lenses for premium iPhones,
offsetting legacy price cuts and lower VR lens shipments. We still keep 7% below VA on
4Q26E revenue (NT$7.34bn, −6% QoQ) reflecting muted iPhone sales and no content

in the iPhone 18 Pro's new variable-aperture lens.
CPO component strategy offers a different route to market from Largan
Unlike Largan's pursuit of complete FAU assembly, Genius is targeting high precision
optical components within the module, including: 1) high-precision prism to collimate
light from the PIC into the fibers, 2) V-groove/key slot to precisely hold the fibers in place,
and 3) MT ferrules (high density plastic connector bundling and aligning fibers). We

believe Genius' customer is one of the key module suppliers on NVIDIA's 2H27 program
targeting over 50% share. Our base case models a complete-FAU market of US$1.85bn
in 2028E and US$4.45bn in 2030E, with Genius base case for US$185m and US$668m
representing 10% and 15% revenue share of the complete-FAU revenue TAM.
Valuation: Maintain Buy with  PT lifted to NT$1,250
We fine-tune our 2026/2027 basic EPS estimates from NT$37.2/NT$41.5 to NT$36.7/NT

$44.0 and introduce 2028E EPS of NT$49.25. We move from P/B to P/E SOTP as the core
business now delivers steadier earnings and CPO warrants separate valuation. Our NT
$1,250 PT comprises NT$745 base business  (15x 2028E EPS of NT$49.25) and NT$505
for CPO (40x base case NT$12.72 EPS in 2028E). Catalysts include qualifications at
leading FAU makers and the board approved 7mn share private private placement with
potential strategic investor (albeit 6% dilution if issued). Our US$1.8bn CPO value

compares to US$7bn implied in our Largan NT$5,000 PT.

### Page 2

Genius Electronic Optical UBS Research
Thesis Map     UBS                    Research     THESIS MAP a guide to our thinking and what´s where in this report
Q: Could Genius' CPO products contribute meaningful revenue in a few years?
Yes, from H227E into 2028E. We see Genius' CPO strategy as feasible and lower-risk than Largan's.
Largan's goal is to supply the complete FAU; Genius is entering at the component level, supplying V-
grooves, MT ferrules and prisms to FAU makers. This lets Genius sell into multiple FAU designs and

integrators, limiting its downside if the market converges on a single winning architecture.
Management believes multi-sourcing is inevitable once volumes scale and has seen early indications
of potentially substantial demand from US optical players, including Corning and Coherent. Genius
has a partnership with FOCI and is pursuing other FAU integrators including we believe Coherent;
management suggested further updates could come in the near term. Some parts already ship in

small volumes, but we do not expect meaningful revenue until H227 or 2028.
Q: Will Genius benefit from further lens technology upgrades in the coming years?
Yes. We expect premium lens participation and product mix to support core revenue growth of 8.0%,
9.7% and 9.1% in 2026-28E. We believe Genius is gaining share from Largan in Apple's periscope
lens supply chain after initially lagging. Apple (70–80% of Genius' sales) sell-through units grew 9%

YoY YTD through August according to Counterpoint, and Genius' 9M26 revenue grew 10% YoY on
a similar trajectory. Largan grew only 2% in 9M26, with Q326 down 11% YoY. For the iPhone 18
series, Genius participates in every lens spec except the variable-aperture upgrade, where it stepped
back because of pricing pressure from Chinese competitors. Management says Genius has the
technical capability and could enter if future specs or design changes improve the opportunity.

Genius offers a combination of an established earnings base from smartphone lens and potential
exposure to the passive components required for optical interconnects. We remain constructive on
the lens business and see CPO as a potentially material addition from 2028. The component strategy
provides several routes to customers, but the valuation should reflect uncertainty over qualified
content, allocation and margins. Our Buy rating and NT$1,250 PT is based  on NT$745 base business

(15x 2028E EPS of NT$49.25) plus NT$505 for the CPO earnings (40x base case NT$12.72 EPS in
2028E). The CPO option for Genius is US$1.8bn vs. US$7bn implied in our Largan NT$5,000 PT.
Genius share gains are showing in reported sales, with YTD sales +10% YoY vs. +2% YoY for Largan.
The company is also participating in every spec except variable aperture.  For CPO, Genius delivered
samples of its prism, V-groove, and MT ferrule to FOCI at the end of May, with initial feedback broadly

on track and no major issues reported to date. Our checks indicate the company is also positioning to
supply one of the leading FAU module suppliers for NVIDIA's CPO program from H227.
At NT$1,060, the shares trade at 24.1x our 2027E core EPS and 21.5x our 2028E core EPS, factoring
some of the CPO opportunity in the multiple rather than in estimates relative to the 10 year average
17x P/E. Applying a 15x valuation to the smartphone lens business + 40x to the CPO option from our

scenario of NT$9.30 EPS in 2028E yields upside from the last close at NT$1,060 to a NT$1,250 share
price.

Genius Electronic Optical UBS Research
Core business intact: periscope gains offset muted smartphone demand
September sales of NT$2.67bn (−2% MoM, +1% YoY) took Q326 to NT$7.82bn (+38%
QoQ, +1% YoY), in-line with VA consensus. Genius grew 10% YoY in 9M26 against 2%
at Largan, which we attribute to share gains in periscope lenses for premium iPhones,
offsetting legacy price cuts and lower VR lens shipments. We remain 7% below VA on
4Q26E revenue (NT$7.34bn, −6% QoQ) reflecting muted iPhone sales and no content

in the iPhone 18 Pro's new variable-aperture lens.

With CPO revenue contribution <5% in 2026 per management guidance, Genius' lens
business should remain its primary earnings anchor. With foldables shipping in 2H, Q3

shipment orders are broadly in line with historical patterns but should be watchful on
near-term demand trends as customers tend to adjust orders quickly in response to new
product sell-through evidenced by Largan's recent cuts. VR contribution has declined
from 5–10% in 2025 to <5% in 2026 on demand slowdown, partially offsetting
strength in phone lens while the mix shift would enhance Genius' overall margin profile.
On the other hand, AR is seeing traction supported by ongoing Ray-Ban shipments with

next-gen products in development.

### Page 4

Genius position in the CPO value chain
Genius is targeting the CPO market as a component supplier while Largan is advancing
from components into full-FAU assembly. Genius is sampling three components for CPO
with FAU makers, including: 1) high-precision prism to redirect light from the PIC into
the fibers, 2) V-groove/key slot to precisely hold the fibers in place, and 3) MT ferrules
(high density plastic connector bundling and aligning fibers). The companies overlap in

capability, with Largan also having PMLA and fiber arrays but not getting into some
components like MT ferrules and V-Grooves.
Genius's optical design, precision tooling, metrology and volume-manufacturing
experience offer it a good entry point for this market. Commercial differentiation will
depend on reproducible optical performance, dimensional tolerances, thermal stability
and customer yield.  It has announced collaboration with FOCI and  we believe has

potential to also work with one of NVIDIA's leading module suppliers for its H227
program.

Annual FAU TAM reaches US$1.85bn in 2028E and US$4.5bn in 2030E in

our base case
We update our FAU (fiber array unit) TAM factoring CPO/NPO penetration into
AI networking switches, NVIDIA multi-rack systems, scale-up on compute and switch
boards, and non-NVIDIA accelerators.  We assume one passive FAU assembly connecting
a fiber array to a photonic engine with one unit per optical engine.
We assume one FAU per optical engine and 32 per switch ASIC in our base case,
matching NVIDIA's 32x3.2T engines on its 102.4T Spectrum-X package. Broadcom's

102.4Tb/s Davisson uses 16 engines at 6.4Tb/s, so our bear case assumes an even mix of
these two designs (24 per ASIC). We note that higher switch bandwidth does not
automatically increase FAU count if engine bandwidth rises at the same rate. Our 2030E
assumption maintains the 32 FAUs per switch based on bandwidth rising to 204.8Tb/s

Our base case for the FAU market sums up 4 opportunities for CPO/NPO attach:
1. Scale-out and scale-across switches
We estimate annual switch ASIC shipments x CPO + NPO penetration x optical engines
per ASIC x FAUs per engine. In 2028E, 25% CPO/NPO penetration or 1.0m 102.4T
switch ASICs at 32 FAUs each produces 8.0m FAUs. In 2030E, 55% of 1.1mn

204.8T switch ASICs at 32 FAUs drives a market size of 19.4mn FAUs.

2. NVIDIA's multi-rack connections for Rubin Ultra and Feynman
NVIDIA at its 2026 GTC San Jose proposed a multi-rack option to network together 8
Oberon NVL72 racks for the Rubin Ultra generation and 8 Kyber NVL144 racks.

Feedback at the time showed  possible transceiver or NPO connections for Rubin Ultra
and CPO connections for Feynman.  Our 2028 base case uses 110,000 racks, 15% multi-
rack adoption and 75% CPO/NPO penetration, giving approximately 12,400 connected
racks.
For 2030E, our rack count falls to 35,000 racks as we assume Kyber would have 4x the
number of GPUs, equivalent to 140k Oberon equivalents. We assume the 35,000 racks
at 55% multi-rack adoption x 80% CPO/NPO attach gives 15,400 connected racks. We

assume about 216 and 576 inter-rack FAUs per rack, respectively, driving a market
opportunity of 2.7mn FAUs in 2028E and 8.9mn in 2030E.

Overall market opportunity: 12.3mn/35.6mn generates a US$1.85bn/US
$4.5bn TAM in 2028/2030E
We sum four pools: scale-out/scale-across switches, NVIDIA multi-rack pods, CPO native
NVIDIA GPUs and non-NVIDIA scale-up.  We add 10%/5% for FAUs lost at downstream
attach and test and for field spares, driving a market size of in our base case of 12.3mn
FAUs in 2028E and 35.6mn in 2030E. At US$150/US$125 per FAU, that is a US$1.85bn

market in 2028E and US$4.5bn in 2030E (bear/bull US$0.5bn/US$3.5bn in 2028E; US
$1.4bn/US$9.0bn in 2030E). The expansion to 2030E comes from wider optical
adoption and greater content in scale-up systems, despite an assumed FAU ASP decline
from US$150 to US$125.

Scenario analysis on Genius CPO opportunity

### Page 7

We assume in base case Genius captures 10% of the TAM in 2028E and 15% by 2030E.
The base case in 2028E is consistent with US$30 of prism, V-groove and ferrule content
in half of all FAUs or US$15 average content spread across the FAU market.  The
2030E bull case (25%) or US$37.50 would require near full content of some of the sub-
components or otherwise ability to integrate more of the solution value by that time.
Our bear case assumes only 1% share of the opportunity in 2028E and 5% of the

opportunity in 2030E, with EPS even by 2030E falling to NT$4 and yielding limited
valuation potential for the shares.

Reiterate Buy and raise PT to NT$1,250 valuing in CPO optionality

We adjust our valuation method from P/B to P/E SOTP to better reflect the company’s
core business and its opportunity within CPO industry while the core smartphone
business is demonstrating more stable earnings to use P/E vs. prior P/B. Our PT at NT
$1,250 is based on NT$745 base business  (15x 2028E EPS of NT$49.25) plus NT$505
for the CPO earnings (40x base case NT$12.72 EPS in 2028E) with catalysts from
upcoming qualifications into leading FAU makers and potential strategic private

placement with board approving 7mn share private placement (albeit 6% dilution if
issued). We note Genius CPO option valued at US$1.8bn vs. US$7bn implied in our
Largan valuation.
Our price target uses 2028 earnings for both businesses, reflecting the modeled start of
meaningful CPO contribution. We apply 15x core EPS of NT$49.25 and 40x CPO EPS of
NT$12.72 for a NT$1,250 price target. The 40x multiple assumes the optical business
can sustain strong growth after its initial ramp; qualification, concentration and margin

risk limit the confidence that can be placed on that earnings stream.
In the upside case, stronger premium lens participation and manufacturing execution lift
2028E core EPS to NT$54.32. Faster optical adoption expands the 2028E FAU market to
US$3.46bn; 15% effective revenue capture and a 31% pretax margin produce
approximately NT$36.88 CPO EPS. Applying 15x to core earnings and 42x to CPO
earnings gives approximately NT$2,365.
In the downside case, weaker lens demand and margin pressure lower core EPS to NT

$44.29. Slower optical adoption leaves a US$0.46bn FAU market, of which Genius
captures 1%, producing only NT$0.29 CPO EPS. Applying 12x and 38x gives
approximately NT$540. The downside is driven by both a weaker core business and a
much smaller optical contribution.

Company Description
Founded in 1989, Genius Electronic Optical (GSEO) is a leading global manufacturer of
camera lenses, providing full service optical solutions, including mobile lenses, automotive
lenses and AR/VR optics. Its production sites are in Taichung (one plant) and Xiamen (two

plants). The Taichung plant mainly manufactures VR lenses, while the Xiamen plants produce
compact lenses for consumer electronics. Optical lens products accounted for 73% of 2023
total revenue, and others include TOF lenses, flash lenses, VR lenses and LED lighting
products. Its major client is Apple and it supplies lenses for iPhones and iPads.
UBS EPS and Consensus EPS
The UBS EPS is an adjusted diluted EPS metric. It is calculated using the UBS analyst's

interpretation of earnings suitable for valuation purposes divided by the diluted number of
shares. This may differ to the way the consensus EPS metric has been calculated.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Our Genius price target is based on PE-based sum-of-the-parts model. Genius' growth is
highly leveraged to the overall handset industry and could be affected by volatility in handset
demand, as well as whether handset OEMs will continue upgrading their camera design.

Genius' margins could be affected by increasing competition and the rise of new
technologies, ASP pressure from customers and fluctuating material and labour costs.
Downside risks include competitors catching up in technology, yield rate issues for new
products and uncertain iPhone demand.
Our price target is based on SOTP valuation on the CPO opportunity and traditional lens
business. We think Largan's downside risks include: 1) ongoing pricing pressure from

smartphone OEM clients and increasingly intense competition among lens suppliers; and 2) a
slower-thanexpected smartphone demand recovery in China, and 3) CPO commercial success
below the market's elevated expectations. We believe the key upside risk is a faster-than-
expected CPO qualification process at Largan. In addition, if the company demonstrates
exceptionally efficient and highly accurate automation capabilities, it could support greater
market share gains within the industry than we currently anticipate.

horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

(ESG) factors into investment process and portfolio construction. Strategies across geographies approach ESG analysis and incorporate the findings in a variety of ways.
Incorporating ESG factors or sustainable investing considerations may inhibit the portfolio manager’s ability to participate in certain investment opportunities that otherwise

would be consistent with its investment objective and other principal investment strategies. The returns on a portfolio incorporating ESG factors or sustainable investing
considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the
investment opportunities available to such portfolios may differ.

## Document 135: 38美元，DeepSeek算了一笔赚钱的账

Source PDF: all report pdf/38美元，DeepSeek算了一笔赚钱的账.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-24
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

DeepSeek 最新披露⼀个震撼数字：单 GPU 每⼩时可创造 38 美元计费产值，年化超 33 万美
元。但这⻔⽣意真的赚钱吗？⼀边是中⼩企业 AI 需求 " 全⾯暴涨 " ，⼀边是 OpenAI 、
Anthropic 被推理成本侵蚀的⽑利率；⼀边是 CoreWeave112% 的营收增速，⼀边是⾼达
6.26 亿美元的季度净亏损。效率提升与商业盈利之间，横亘着⼀道尚未解答的命题。
摘要：
10 ⽉ 20 ⽇， DeepSeek 开源推理框架 DeepInfer 时，公布了⼀个⼗分吸引眼球的数字：单个集群
每天可以处理 155 万亿 Token ，如果按照 DeepSeek API 的⾕时价格计算，平均每 GPU ⼩时对应
38 美元的收⼊。
这是根据 Token 处理量和公开 API 价格折算出的理论计费收⼊，实际情况要复杂得多。但这个数
字依然太有吸引⼒：如果粗暴按照线性外推，按每天 24 ⼩时、全年 365 天计算，⼀张 GPU 的年
化计费产值超过 33 万美元。
过去⼏年， AI ⾏业⼀直在讨论训练⼀个前沿模型需要多少钱，英伟达的 GPU 有多贵，云⼚商⼜

投⼊了多少资⾦建设数据中⼼。但这些昂贵的计算设备买回来之后，究竟能够⽣产多少可以卖
钱的智能？抛开眼花缭乱的 “ ⾦融⼿段 ” ，企业真能赚到的，⼜有多少钱？
DeepSeek 的 38 美元，提供了⼀个难得的观察窗⼝。在资本市场再次对 AI 巨额投⼊的回报提出质
疑的时候，产业⼀线的反馈却很乐观。 “ 业内 ” 和 “ 业外 ” ，产⽣了巨⼤的分歧。
⼀位经营 Token 中转业务的创业者告诉腾讯科技，最近⼏个⽉，中⼩企业的 AI 需求正在 “ 全⾯暴
涨 ”，他能从客⼾的实际使⽤情况中感受到这⼀变化。今年七⼋⽉还只有少数公司在尝试的事
情，如今同⾏已经普遍在做。⼀些原本有数据安全顾虑的企业也开始私有化部署模型。他也在
认真考虑 “ 扩产 ” 。
另外⼀位 AI 应⽤领域的创业者态度也⼗分鲜明： “ 英伟达的股票，我还是会继续买。 ” 他接触的 B 端
企业正在加快引⼊ Agent ，从⽇常运营到客⼾服务，越来越多业务开始 AI 化。
但他也判断，中美 AI 市场短期内可能经历⼀个 “ ⼩低⾕ ” 。此前模型突破、⽣产率提升和 AI 替代⼯

作的消息不断推⾼市场预期，当资本市场开始要求 AI 在真⾦⽩银上交出成绩时，企业应⽤的实际
成果可能还没有跟上，市场热情也会随之回落。
这或许正是当下 AI 产业⾯临的⽭盾：⼀线需求正在增⻓，但从应⽤落地到形成⾜以⽀撑巨额算⼒
投⼊的商业回报，中间还有⼀段距离。
DeepSeek 的计算提供了另⼀个值得关注的信号，即使按照⾕时价格，⾼度优化的推理系统仍然
能够实现相当可观的单位 GPU 计费产值。
那么，随着推理效率不断提⾼，如果有⾜够多的⽤⼾持续消耗 Token ， AI 是否已经可以成为⼀⻔
赚钱的⽣意？答案可能⽐想象中乐观，但也更复杂。
⼀张 GPU ，每⼩时 38 美元
DeepSeek 在 9 ⽉ 10 ⽇发布的 V4.1 Flash 中，已经披露了不少推理效率优化的技术细节。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

按照官⽅技术报告，这款模型拥有 5520 亿总参数，采⽤混合专家（ MoE ）架构和新的 Causal
Encoder–Decoder 设计，处理输⼊时约激活 80 亿参数，⽣成输出时约激活 160 亿参数。
模型针对⻓上下⽂和 Agent 场景优化了缓存结构。按照论⽂披露，全局 KV Cache 占⽤降低到上
⼀代的约四分之⼀，⻓期保存的缓存占⽤减少到约⼋分之⼀。每 Token 的全局 KV Cache 约为 890
字节，⽀持最⾼ 100 万 Token 上下⽂。
这些优化直接影响⻓上下⽂推理的显存占⽤、数据传输和服务成本。
10 ⽉ 20 ⽇开源的 DeepInfer ，进⼀步公开了 DeepSeek 如何将这些技术整合为⼤规模在线推理服
务。
它集成了此前陆续开源的 FlashMLA 、 DeepGEMM 、 DeepEP 、 TileKernels 等组件，同时引⼊专
家并⾏、推测解码、异步调度等机制。
图： DeepSeek 在线推理系统⽰意图
DeepSeek 在官⽅部署问答中重点介绍了三项⼯程原则：适配不同⼚商的加速硬件，将 Prefill 与

Decode 分开部署，以及尽可能⻓期保留 KV Cache ，减少重复计算。
在⽣产环境中， Prefill 可以部署在普通 8 卡服务器上，⽽ Decode 采⽤ 64 ⾄ 128 卡超节点，通过⼤
规模专家并⾏提⾼吞吐量。 DeepSeek 还提出将 KV Cache 保留 72 ⼩时以上，以适应 Agent 反复
调⽤相同上下⽂的需求。
这些优化的核⼼⽬标，是在满⾜延迟要求的前提下，让同样数量的 GPU 服务更多请求。
不过，这次开源并不意味着 DeepSeek 已经公开完整⽣产环境中的所有组件。
官⽅ README ⽂档指出， KV Cache 系统、视觉编码与 Prefill/Decode 分离服务编排、跨数据并
⾏副本的负载均衡等，仍需要外部补⻬。部分组件与 DeepSeek 内部基础设施深度耦合，因此尚
未完整开源。
DeepInfer ⽬前也主要⾯向 DeepSeek-V4.1 Flash 。其他模型如果在 MoE 专家组织、注意⼒结
构、数值精度或硬件配置上存在较⼤差异，很难直接照搬。

因此， 38 美元并不代表任何企业下载 DeepInfer 、部署⼀批 GPU ，就能获得同样的收⼊表现。
DeepSeek 也没有完整公开计算这项指标所需的集群硬件构成、成本账单、付费流量⽐例及收⼊
确认⽅式。

### Page 3

但它⾄少说明，在特定模型与硬件条件下，推理⼯程优化能够显著提⾼计算设备的潜在计费产
值。
低价出售 Token ，与⼀张 GPU 产⽣较⾼的计费收⼊，并不⽭盾。
关键在于，⼀⼩时内究竟能够处理多少有效 Token ，以及有多少 Token 真正可以向客⼾收费。
如果推理可以赚钱，为什么模型公司还在亏损？
如果推理效率已经可以做到如此程度，为什么⼀些⼤模型公司仍然需要不断融资，甚⾄持续亏
损？
⼀个重要原因是，训练前沿模型和出售推理服务，本来就是两笔不同的账。
2026 年斯坦福⼤学《 AI Index 》引⽤研究机构 Epoch AI 的数据，估算了 OpenAI 和 Anthropic 在模
型研发与推理服务⽅⾯的计算⽀出。
2025 年， OpenAI 计算⽀出约 163 亿美元，其中研发相关计算⽀出 83 亿美元，推理⽀出 80 亿美
元，两者⼏乎各占⼀半。
2024 年， OpenAI 的相应⽀出分别为 40 亿美元和 18 亿美元。也就是说，⼀年之间，推理计算⽀出
增⻓了约 3.4 倍，⽽研发计算⽀出增⻓约⼀倍。

Anthropic 在 2025 年的计算⽀出估计为 68 亿美元，其中研发相关⽀出 41 亿美元，推理⽀出 27 亿美
元。研发仍然占据较⼤⽐例。
这些数字说明，前沿模型公司的算⼒投⼊结构正在变化。随着商业⽤⼾增加，推理已成为越来
越重要的成本来源，但研发开⽀并没有因为推理业务扩张⽽消失。
⼀家追逐 SOTA 的模型公司，需要同时承担持续训练和⼤规模推理服务的成本。前者包括预训
练、后训练、强化学习、数据处理、模型评测及⼤量实验，后者则需要维持在线服务的算⼒、
容量和稳定性。
Epoch AI 特别指出，研发计算⽀出并不全部⽤于最后⼀次正式训练。⼤量资源实际上消耗在实
验、研究和模型迭代过程中。因此，某个模型公开的单次训练成本，不能简单视为⼀家前沿模
型公司的全部研发成本。
图源： Stanford HAI / Epoch AI ， 2026 年

### Page 4

商业化带来的另⼀层压⼒，是⽤⼾越多，推理成本也可能越⾼。
《 The Information 》今年 2 ⽉报道， OpenAI 在 2025 年的⽑利率约为 33% ，低于 2024 年的 40% ，
也低于此前 46% 的预测。 Anthropic 对 2025 年⽑利率的预期约为 40% ，虽然较 2024 年的负⽑利状
态改善明显，仍⽐其早先⽬标低 10 个百分点。
报道指出，两家公司⽑利率低于预期的重要原因之⼀，是实际推理成本⾼于原先估计。
这与 DeepSeek 的 38 美元看起来形成了⽭盾：⼀边是持续改善的推理效率，另⼀边却是被推理成
本侵蚀的⽑利率。
实际上，它们反映的是同⼀⻔⽣意的两个⽅⾯。
单位 GPU 能够⽣产多少 Token ，是供给效率；公司需要投⼊多少 GPU ，才能满⾜不同客⼾的延
迟、上下⽂⻓度、稳定性和峰值需求，则决定实际经营成本。
即使单位 Token 成本下降，重度⽤⼾增加、任务执⾏时间延⻓、模型调⽤次数上升，也可能使总
推理⽀出迅速增⻓。尤其在固定⽉费的产品中，客⼾⽀付的价格未必随着 Token 消耗同步增加。

想想每天挖空⼼思发 “ 重置卡 ” 的 Tibo 。
图： OpenAI 产品与平台负责⼈ Tibo （ Thibault Sottiaux ）于 10 ⽉ 11 ⽇在 X 平台连续发布有关
Reset 的动态，其中 “Day 6 / Reset” 获得约 290 万次浏览。
⼀家模型公司的推理服务可以拥有正⽑利，整个公司却仍然因为持续研发、基础设施和经营投
⼊⽽亏损。
反过来，如果不承担前沿模型训练，专⻔经营推理服务，⽣意的成本结构就完全不同。
不训练模型，只卖 Token 能不能赚钱？
AI 产业链中已经出现⼀类与前沿模型实验室不同的公司。
它们不以训练最强的基础模型为核⼼业务，⽽是部署 DeepSeek 、 Qwen 、 Llama 等开放权重模
型，通过多芯⽚集群、推理框架、模型优化和调度系统，向开发者与企业销售 API 调⽤、专属推
理实例或私有化部署服务。

### Page 5

对于这些公司来说，模型本⾝未必需要从头研发，成本主要来⾃ GPU 采购或租赁、机房与电
⼒、⽹络和存储、推理系统研发、模型适配，以及销售、运维和客⼾⽀持。
其中最关键的三个变量，是单位 GPU 产值、实际付费利⽤率和综合运营成本。
假设⼀家推理服务商每 GPU ⼩时的完全负担成本为 10 美元，⽽它能够以 DeepSeek 披露的 38 美
元作为满负载计费产值的参考，那么理论上只要实现约 26.3% 的有效计费产出，便可以覆盖这
10 美元成本。
如果有效计费产出达到 50% ，收⼊相当于每⼩时 19 美元，扣除 10 美元后还剩 9 美元。
当然，这只是⽤于说明商业敏感性的假设。实际系统的吞吐量、并发和延迟具有复杂关系，低
负载下的收⼊不⼀定与满负载表现呈线性变化。 10 美元也不是 DeepSeek 披露的真实成本，更不
能将计算结果当作其实际⽑利率。
但这种测算说明，推理系统效率⾜够⾼时，商业回报可能具有较⼤的空间。问题是， GPU 本⾝
并不便宜，购买或者租赁 GPU ，同样不意味着⽴即拥有稳定收⼊。

CoreWeave 提供了⼀个可以对照的真实案例。
2026 年第⼆季度， CoreWeave 收⼊ 25.75 亿美元，同⽐增⻓约 112% ，调整后 EBITDA 达到 15.10
亿美元，利润率为 59% 。
但在美国通⽤会计准则下，公司当季仍净亏损 6.26 亿美元。问题出在算⼒⽣意⾼昂的资产和资⾦
成本上。 CoreWeave 需要投⼊⼤量资⾦采购 GPU 及配套设备，相关资产随着使⽤逐年折旧；同
时，公司依靠债务融资⽀持扩张，还要承担⾼额利息。 2026 年第⼆季度，其折旧及摊销费⽤达
到 13.93 亿美元，净利息⽀出⾼达 6.4 亿美元。
折旧反映的是已经购⼊的算⼒设备在使⽤期间逐步计⼊的成本，利息是为这些投资融资所付出
的代价。即使 GPU 租赁带来了可观收⼊，这两项负担仍可能⼤幅压缩利润。
CoreWeave 的财报也说明，算⼒利⽤率⾼、收⼊增⻓快，并不意味着经营算⼒就⼀定赚钱。
更复杂的是，推理算⼒并不是⼀个完全同质化的市场。
DeepSeek 能够通过深度优化获得较⾼单位 GPU 产值，不意味着普通推理服务商可以⽤相同的硬

件、相同的价格获得同样表现。
因此，推理服务可能是⼀⻔具有可观⽑利空间的⽣意，但它并不会⾃动成为⾼利润⽣意。⼯程
能⼒影响成本下限，客⼾需求和定价权决定收⼊上限，⽽资本成本影响最终能留下多少利润。
此外，推理市场还⾯临持续降价的压⼒。
DeepSeek 降低单位 Token 成本之后，也在通过更低的 API 价格将⼀部分效率收益传递给⽤⼾。
其他公司⼀旦采⽤更⾼效的算法和系统，也可能参与价格竞争。
推理效率提升创造的收益，最终未必全部留给卖 Token 的公司。
AI 利润版图，谁能赚钱？

### Page 6

从英伟达、台积电、 SK 海⼒⼠，到数据中⼼和云服务商， AI 基础设施投资已经为部分上游企业
带来了可观的收⼊和利润。
但对于经营推理服务的企业来说，设备买回来之后，仍然需要不断寻找客⼾和任务，才能持续
创造收⼊。
⽬前， Coding 是商业化最清晰的 AI 应⽤市场之⼀，但即使在这个付费意愿较强的领域，盈利也
并不容易。
《 The Information 》披露， Cursor 在截⾄ 2026 年 1 ⽉的季度中，⽑利率⼀度为 -23% ；如果将免
费⽤⼾的推理费⽤全部计⼊销售成本，⽑利率约为 -31% 。虽然此后⽑利率已经转正，但⾼昂的
模型调⽤成本，仍是 AI 应⽤需要⾯对的问题。
消费应⽤的情况更加分化。 10 ⽉ 12 ⽇，曾经的明星 AI 陪伴公司 Character.AI 申请破产保护。根据
破产⽂件及公开报道，公司当时仍拥有约 1200 万⽉活⽤⼾， 2026 年 8 ⽉收⼊达到 530 万美元，但
每⽉经营亏损仍有 800 万⾄ 900 万美元。
仅 2026 年前⼋个⽉， Character.AI 就花费了 5610 万美元购买算⼒，占运营费⽤约 45% 。即使转

向更便宜的 GPU 和开放权重模型，并削减⼈员开⽀，也未能实现盈利。法律诉讼和监管压⼒同
样加剧了公司的困境。
但是， AI 原⽣应⽤并⾮⽆法盈利。 Midjourney 创始⼈ David Holz 在今年 3 ⽉接受《 The
Information 》采访时表⽰，公司 2023 年收⼊已显著超过 2 亿美元，此后继续增⻓，并且保持盈
利。
但它也不是所有 AI 应⽤的普遍样本。图像⽣成拥有相对明确的使⽤场景和付费价值，⽽全天候运
⾏的个⼈ Agent ，可能需要持续调⽤模型、使⽤⼯具和重新规划任务，成本结构更加复杂。
从整体市场看，需求实际上正在快速增⻓。
Menlo Ventures 在 2026 年 9 ⽉发布的《 State of Consumer AI 》报告估计，全球消费者 AI ⽀出已
达到 400 亿美元，是 2025 年 120 亿美元的三倍多。
但值得注意的是，美国成年⼈ AI 使⽤率只从 2025 年的 61% 上升到 64% 。消费者 AI ⽀出的⾼速增
⻓，主要来⾃已有⽤⼾使⽤得更深⼊，付费更多。新增⽤⼾有限。

图： Menlo Ventures 消费 AI ⽀出图

### Page 7

Menlo Ventures 合伙⼈ Amy Wu Martin 指出，今年令⼈意外的变化，是消费者 AI 市场在没有迎来
⼤量新⽤⼾的情况下实现了三倍增⻓。推动增⻓的，是重度⽤⼾将 AI 带⼊更多⽣活场景，并开始
允许 AI 代表⾃⼰采取⾏动。
现在，更重要的是⼀个⽤⼾每天有多少任务交给 AI ，以及每项任务能够创造多少实际价值。这也
是 Personal Agent （ PA ）重新成为⼤⼚和创业公司共同关注⽅向的原因之⼀。
最近推出的 Duet 、 Dots 、 Muse 、 Grokbot 以及⼀批独⽴创业公司的产品，虽然在⼊⼝、记忆、
权限体系和执⾏⽅式上存在不同，但都在尝试扩⼤ AI 能够承接的个⼈任务范围。
如果 Agent 能够持续处理邮件、安排⼯作、操作软件、完成采购、协调不同服务， AI 调⽤便可能
从⽤⼾主动发起的短时会话，转向围绕实际任务展开的⻓流程执⾏。
Agent 之间的协作，以及越来越多由机器直接发起的⼯具调⽤和服务请求，也可能进⼀步扩⼤推
理消费市场。
不过，更多 Token 消耗本⾝并不代表商业成功。

⼀家 PA 公司如果每⽉向⽤⼾收取 20 美元，却需要花费 30 美元购买模型调⽤和其他服务，那么它
可能帮助上游模型商扩⼤收⼊，⾃⼰却仍然⽆法盈利。
同样，模型越来越⾼效，也可能让过去需要⼤量 Token 的任务变得便宜。即使 Agent 数量⼤幅增
⻓，市场总收⼊也不⼀定以相同⽐例增⻓。
模型公司希望扩⼤推理消费市场，应⽤公司希望以更低的推理成本完成任务，⽤⼾关⼼实际完
成的⼯作值多少钱。
DeepSeek 的 38 美元，展⽰了在特定条件下，⾼效率⽣产 Token 所具有的潜在收⼊能⼒。
过去⼏年，资本市场为 AI 投⼊了巨额资⾦，芯⽚、存储和算⼒基础设施率先从中受益。随着推理
系统继续进步，模型公司和应⽤公司⼀直在⾯对另外⼀道更复杂的商业命题：如果越来越多企
业都能以更低的成本⽣产智能，谁能找到⾜够多愿意购买智能的客⼾？
还有更难的问题：如果 AI 是⼀个新的时代， token 承载的 “ 智能 ” ，要创造什么增量？ AI Native 的
核⼼逻辑，还在⼀⽚混沌中等待。
本⽂来源：腾讯科技

### Page 8

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

## Document 184: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점불확실성보다 성장_3801b631

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점불확실성보다 성장_3801b631.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
3분기 실적 컨센서스 상회 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 3분기 매출액은 1조 4,903억 원(+18.5% Y oY), 
영업이익은 6,401억 원(+1.0% Y oY, 영업이익률 43.0%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 각각 
10%, 5% 상회했다. 1~4공장의 풀가동이 이어진 가운데 5공장의 인증용 생산배치 
물량과 미국 락빌 공장 매출(약 1,000억 원)이 처음으로 반영되며 외형 성장을 
견인했다. 매출원가율은 50.4%로 전년 동기(41.4%), 전분기(47.0%) 대비 
상승했는데, 락빌 공장과 5공장의 초기 물량 반영에 따른 것으로 판단한다. 향후 
물량 상승에 따라 매출원가율은 감소할 것으로 예상한다. 그럼에도 EBITDA 
마진율은 50.4%로 높은 수준을 유지하며 7,515억 원을 달성했다. 순이익은 원/달러 
환율 하락에 따른 외화환산손실이 반영되며 3,911억 원을 기록했다.  
환율 하락에도 가이던스 상향하며 성장 궤도 유지 
2026년 연간 매출액은 5조 6,133억 원(+23.2% Y oY), 영업이익은 2조 4,605억 
원(+18.9% YoY)으로 전망한다. 최근 원/달러 환율 하락에도 이번 실적발표에서 
상향 제시한 연간 매출 성장률 20% 이상은 충분히 달성 가능할 것으로 판단한다. 
4분기에는 락빌 공장의 매출 약 1,000억 원과 5공장 가동 확대에 더해, 5월 
파업으로 이연된 물량의 매출 인식이 성장에 기여할 전망이다.  
투자의견 매수 및 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
비만 치료제 CDMO 진출을 위한 폴리펩타이드 그룹 인수는 공개매수 응모율 
87.88%로 최소 조건을 충족해 11월 말 완료를 앞두고 있어 신규 성장 동력은 
확보된 것으로 판단한다. 노사 리스크도 법원의 쟁의행위 제한 확대에 이어 노조가 
2차 총파업을 잠정 연기하며 크게 완화됐다. 미국 관세 불확실성으로 부진했던 신규 
수주 모멘텀은 올해 연말부터 내년에 걸쳐 강화될 것으로 전망하며, 불확실성 
해소와 성장 기대감을 반영할 시점이라 판단한다.

동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

### Page 2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -2.62 điểm tương ứng -0.15%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Dệt may, Công nghệ và Máy móc là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất trong 
tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -13,987 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã HDB, PNJ và VJC; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-30 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng 1,267 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua nhưng đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -2,363 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 3,726 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -2,702 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam trải qua một tuần đi ngang giằng co, giảm nhẹ và đóng cửa ở 1.735 điểm 
với thanh khoản ở mức thấp, và nhà đầu tư nước ngoài bán ròng cả 5 phiên trong tuần. 
Thông tin vĩ mô tích cực với tăng trưởng GDP quý 3 đạt mức gần 10% là điểm sáng trong 
tuần qua, tuy đã không đủ nâng đỡ thị trường chung. Thay vào đó, tâm lý thị trường thận 
trọng trước số liệu lạm phát ở mức cao, đợt tăng giá xăng dầu trong nước và mặt bằng lãi 
suất trong nước chưa có dấu hiệu hạ nhiệt. Diễn biến tăng của giá dầu thế giới, và lợi suất 
trái phiếu Chính phủ Mỹ cũng là các yếu tố gây áp lực lên thị trường chung. Tâm lý thận 
trọng còn thể hiện qua áp lực bán giải chấp ở một số cổ phiếu riêng lẻ như PNJ, SHB..., dù 
một vài nhóm trụ như dầu khí, tiêu dùng có thời điểm đóng vai trò nâng đỡ chỉ số.

Trần Đức Anh 
Giám đốc Kinh tế Vĩ mô & Chiến lược thị trường 
anhtd@kbsec.com.vn 
07/10/2026

Triển vọng TTCK 4Q2026 
Tích lũy trong vùng nhiễu động

Chúng tôi dự báo vùng điểm hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm 2026 
xuống 1,800 đi ểm, giảm mạnh từ mức 1,950 đi ểm đưa ra trong báo cáo gần nhất. 
Vùng điểm này tương ứng:

- Tăng trưởng EPS 2026 so với cùng kỳ của thị trường chung ở mức 33% 
(tăng mạnh so với mức 25% của báo cáo gần nhất), chủ yếu nhờ kỳ vọng 
vào các chính sách thúc đẩy kinh tế của Chính phủ, và lợi nhuận tăng 
trưởng đột biến ở VIC (xem thêm phần Triển vọng hoạt động kinh doanh).

- Mức P/E hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm ở mức 13.2 (giảm 
nhẹ so với mức 13.5 thời điểm hiện tại - phản ánh quan điểm các yếu tố 
rủi ro sẽ tiếp tục duy trì). Đây là mức định giá thấp so với bình quân 5 năm 
gần nhất ở 15.1. Nếu loại bỏ yếu tố đột biến ở nhóm Vingroup, chỉ số P/E 
điều chỉnh hiện đã rơi xuống 10.9, thấp hơn các đáy gần nhất năm 2020, 
2022 và 2025. Trong bối cảnh kinh tế Việt Nam duy trì tốc độ tăng trưởng 
cao, lợi nhuận các doanh nghiệp niêm yết liên tục bứt phá, vùng định giá 
này mở ra cơ hội đầu tư trung/dài hạn. Chúng tôi cho rằng ngay khi các rủi 
ro vĩ mô quốc tế (xoay quanh vấn đề Iran), và trong nước (xoay quanh vấn 
đề lãi suất) hạ nhiệt, thị trường chung sẽ có cơ hội tái định giá lên mức cao 
hơn đáng kể so với thời điểm hiện tại. Dù vậy, khác với báo cáo gần nhất 
vào tháng 7, ở báo cáo này, chúng tôi đẩy lui thời điểm các yếu tố rủi ro vĩ 
mô hạ nhiệt sang năm 2027, đồng nghĩa với quan điểm thận trọng hơn về 
thị trường cho quý 4 2026.

Cụ thể, đối với vấn đề lãi suất, áp lực lạm phát lớn dần, đi kèm nhu cầu vốn cao phục 
vụ hoạt động đầu tư cơ sở hạ tầng sẽ khiến mặt bằng lãi suất duy trì ở mức cao cho 
phần còn lại của năm 2026 (dù thanh khoản hệ thống ghi nhận sự cải thiện đáng kể 
trong 1 quý gần đây). Đối với vấn đề xung đột Trung Đông, các cuộc đàm phán vẫn 
đang bế tắc và ít có kh ả năng dòng ch ảy dầu mỏ tại khu vực này được nối lại ngay 
trong năm nay.

Chủ đề đầu tư chúng tôi l ựa chọn cho 4Q2026 là đ ầu tư công, chính sách ti ền tệ hỗ 
trợ, và xung đột Iran.

Xem đầy đủ tại đây.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành mẫu nến Doji trên khung đồ thị tuần sau hai tuần giảm điểm liên tiếp, 
cho thấy áp lực bán đã phần nào được kiểm soát. Trên khung ngày, nỗ lực hồi phục đầu 
tuần gặp lực cản tại đường SMA50 khiến VNINDEX quay đầu giảm điểm, tiếp tục chớm phá 
đáy ngắn hạn; tuy nhiên vùng hỗ trợ gần đã tạo hiệu ứng thu hút lực cầu bắt đáy, giúp chỉ 
số thu hẹp đà giảm trong phiên cuối tuần. Sau nhịp điều chỉnh khá tiêu cực từ vùng đỉnh 
đầu tháng 9, VNINDEX đang bước vào giai đoạn tìm kiếm điểm cân bằng trở lại và diễn biến 
giằng co điều chỉnh có thể còn kéo dài thêm trong các phiên kế tiếp. Tuy nhiên khi xu 
hướng tăng ngắn hạn tính từ vùng đáy cuối tháng 7 vẫn đang được bảo lưu, chỉ số được kỳ 
vọng sẽ có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại tại điểm đỡ gần quanh 1710 (đây cũng là chốt chặn cần 
giữ để bảo vệ được xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn). 
Kháng cự xa: 1820 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1775 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1710 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1670 (+-15)

### Page 5

5

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua.

## Document 274: Federal Reserve preview_ A pause before a December hike

Source PDF: all report pdf/Federal Reserve preview_ A pause before a December hike.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Despite hot inﬂation prints in September and the Fed having
signalled that further action to tame inﬂation will likely be
needed, markets and economists are united in expecting the
central bank to leave interest rates on hold at the 28 October
FOMC meeting. Ongoing concerns about elevated price
pressures mean we do expect a December interest rate rise

James Knightley, Padhraic Garvey, CFA and Chris Turner
The Fed looks set to hold rates steady at the October FOMC meeting before
hiking again in December
Fed ofﬁcials play for time
The immediate reaction to the surprise September 0.3% month-on-
month core CPI print was to send the chances of an October rate
hike above 50%. But this didn’t last long. Earlier comments from
Federal Reserve Vice Chairs Philip Jeﬀerson and Michelle Bowman
and Governor Chris Waller, signalling no rush to decide on the next
policy move, quickly calmed markets. The fact that Fed Chair Kevin
Warsh subsequently chose not to talk up the chances of action
when he spoke at the IMF meetings in Bangkok, unlike at the

### Page 2

Jackson Hole Conference before the September hike, further
dampened the perceived chances of action.
While it is true to say that recent jobs numbers have been
disappointing and interest rate-sensitive sectors, such as the
housing market, are experiencing increasing pain, the economy is
growing robustly, and inﬂation remains above target. Fed oﬃcials
themselves have made it clear that they think more tightening of
monetary policy is going to be required to bring inﬂation to heel.

Given that the latest Federal Reserve Summary of Economic
Projections showed four FOMC members projecting 50bp of rate
hikes between now and the end of the year, the decision is unlikely
to be unanimous. Beth Hammack, Neel Kashkari and Lorie Logan
all voted to hike in July when the rest of the committee wanted to
hold steady. All three could again dissent in favour of hiking.
The press release is likely to be brief, and we also expect another
short and snappy press conference from Chair Warsh. He's likely to

keep things quite matter-of-fact given his lack of enthusiasm for
forward guidance. We expect his message to be that the economy
remains in decent shape, unemployment is low, and inﬂation is
above target. The policy changes being made will be determined
by the trends in incoming data, with oﬃcials currently thinking that
they probably have more work to do.

versus the Fed funds policy rate

Looking towards December
We will have two jobs reports and one inﬂation print ahead of the
December decision. The November CPI will be released the day after
the Fed meeting, and oﬃcials will almost certainly get to see it. As
such, they will have more information than the market does,

meaning a surprise can’t be completely ruled out.
We are forecasting a 25bp rate hike at the December meeting
(leaving the Fed funds target rate range at 4-4.25%) followed by
a prolonged pause through to December 2027, when we have that
December 2026 hike being reversed. This is in line with the current
consensus forecast, while the Fed’s own projections (its central
forecast) see one hike this year with rates left unchanged
throughout 2027.
While the sharp move higher in Treasury yields and the ﬁrmer

dollar are tightening ﬁnancial conditions to a degree, our forecast
path for the Fed assumes that in the couple of months following
the midterm elections we see an agreement that improves the ﬂow
of energy from the Persian Gulf, but signiﬁcantly, also allows for a
sharp reduction in shipping and insurance costs. It's the latter point
that is crucial to delivering lower pump prices at a time when
reﬁneries are working at capacity. If correct, this should

meaningfully ease inﬂation pressures, and with US wage growth
(the biggest cost input for corporate America) looking very benign
at just 3% and the largest component of the inﬂation basket –
housing at 35% by weight – set to experience more disinﬂation, we

### Page 4

believe overall US inﬂation can fall back to 2% in the second half of
2027.
The risk to our view is clearly tilted towards a longer-lasting conﬂict
rather than one that is resolved imminently. This means we would
not rule out additional hike(s) in 1Q 2027, but with households
experiencing a greater squeeze on spending power (weak wage
growth amid rising energy costs) and bond yields likely pushing
even higher – leading to new cycle highs for mortgage rates and

corporate borrowing costs – the risks of a weaker economy will
increase. As such, in this more stressed economic scenario,
eventual cuts to policy rates will likely be swifter and more
substantial in 2H 2027 than in our base case.
The Fed to leave well enough alone and let
Treasuries do their own thing
A solid performance for US Treasuries in the past week has acted to
reduce the prior tightening impulse that had gripped the market.
The 10yr yield below 5% obviously contrasts dramatically with the

prior break above 5.25%. We’re not convinced the more recent
downward lurch is persisting. But its genesis lends itself to a softer
approach from the FOMC at this meeting.
The risk-oﬀ tone creates a heightened risk proﬁle for the US
economy, raising the probability that the Fed holds at this meeting.
The battering that the French bond market has been taking is
another important impulse, as it heightens the broader sense of
bond angst. And at the margin, it can add to systemic pressure.

This is not the type of environment where the Fed would be inclined
to add to concerns by tightening policy. It’s the perfect meeting to
keep rates on hold.
Whether we remain below 5% for the 10yr yield is a whole other
question. We're minded to expect it to snap back above, as we still
have inﬂation, ﬁscal deﬁcit, and AI-boom impulses at play. The Fed
will no doubt remind us of these ongoing pressures. Plus, a break
back above 5% will likely need a degree of calm in the risk space

ﬁrst. We think it should ultimately move back above 5%; the timing,

We’re likely still some way from the Fed spelling out a plan to shrink
its balance sheet, which we know remains a policy objective for
Chair Warsh. We expect recommendations from the various
committees to surface closer to the turn of the year. Meanwhile,
liquidity circumstances are stable, with repo behaving far better
than it did a year ago.

Warsh FOMC meetings have set the dollar
tone since June
The dollar should be able to hold on to most of its September
gains, as Fed communication is likely to remain supportive of
higher US yields. If the dollar is to correct lower, a renewed tech-led
risk-oﬀ move rather than a dovish shift in Fed messaging is the
more likely catalyst.
After a sharp 4% run higher through September and into early
October, the dollar has entered a more consolidative phase. Rate

diﬀerentials are the most important driver at the moment, and
these have moved slightly against the dollar this month as the US
tech sell-oﬀ has pared back Fed tightening expectations.
Broadening out the lens, it looks like Warsh-led FOMC meetings
have set the four-to-six-week trend for the dollar this year. June’s
meeting was hawkish, July’s was seen as dovish, and the Fed
delivered a hawkish hike in September.
We suspect that Warsh has learned from the criticism he received

over July's FOMC meeting, where he implied that the bond market
was doing the tightening (read as the Fed would not have to). That
probably means he will not be drawn into concluding that wider
credit spreads and softer equity prices mean tighter ﬁnancial
conditions now require fewer Fed hikes.
Assuming that Warsh sticks to the script that monetary policy
credibility remains the Fed’s top priority, short-dated US yields can
remain well-supported, and the dollar can largely hold on to the

gains it has made since early September. And the euro looks like it

Westpac New Zealand Economics
Stopping for gas
• We estimate that New Zealand consumer
prices rose by 0.7% in the September quarter,
with a moderation in fuel costs balanced
against increases in council rates, building
costs and food prices.
• The annual inflation rate is expected to drop
back to 3.7% (down from 4.1% in the year
to  June).
• Measures of core inflation are expected to
remain firm, with some closely watched
measures expected to rise to the top of the

RBNZ’s target band.
• Our forecast is a little below the RBNZ’s last
published forecast for 3.9% annual inflation.

We expect the September quarter inflation report (out
on 22 October) will show that New Zealand consumer
prices rose 0.7% over the past three months. That would
see the annual inflation rate slipping back to 3.7%, down
from 4.1% in the year to June. However, as we discuss

in our recent forecast update, that’s likely to be just a
temporary reprieve with the recent rise in oil prices set to
push inflation up again over the coming months.
16 Oct 2026 | Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5668 | +64 21 710 852 | satish.ranchhod@westpac.co.nz
ECONOMIC BULLETIN
CPI preview, September quarter 2026 –
Thursday 22 October, 10:45am.

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
Key drivers of quarterly inflation.
Underpinning our forecast for 0.7% inflation in the
September quarter are some big price movements in
both directions.
The largest move in prices this quarter has actually been
a sharp fall in fuel costs. Fuel prices aren’t low by any
stretch of the imagination. However, average prices for
both petrol and diesel over the September quarter were
lower than in the June quarter when prices rocketed

higher in the wake of the Middle East conflict – see the
chart below. In terms of specifics, the average price for 91
unleaded was around 6% lower in September than in the
June quarter, while diesel prices were close to 16% lower.
Fuel spending accounts for around 4% of the CPI, and
these moves have taken 0.4ppts off inflation this quarter.

While prices at the pump weren’t as high as they were in
the June quarter, fuel costs are still painfully high. And
that’s been bleeding through into higher prices for other
goods and services. Of note, the September quarter
saw large increases in passenger transport costs, with
international airfares up 14% and domestic airfares up

nearly 8%. Together those prices account for around 2%
of the CPI.
We also expect building costs (10% of the CPI) will rise by
1.6% this quarter following a similarly chunky rise in June.
Again, much of that rise has been related to higher global
oil prices which have been flowing through to higher
operating and material costs. That’s occurred at the same
time as the amount of planned construction work has
been rising.
The September quarter also sees the annual increases

in local council rates and charges for services like water
supply (around 4% of the CPI). We expect these costs will
be up around 7.4% this year, continuing the run of large
increases seen in previous years.
Lastly, the past few months saw a 1.0% rise in food prices
(18% of the CPI), largely as a result of the seasonal rise in
the price for fresh vegetables.
On the downside, we’ve seen continued softness in
housing rents, which are the largest component of

the CPI, accounting for 10% of the basket. With low
population growth and ample supply, rents have
effectively been flat since the start of this year. In annual
terms, rental inflation is the lowest it’s been in more
than two decades, with regions like Wellington seeing
outright falls.

Where could we be surprised?
There are some key areas where we see the risk
of a downside surprise. The first is the price for
communications equipment, like mobile phones. After
a large rise in the June quarter, we could see a sizeable

drop in prices this quarter.
There could also be softness in used car prices, especially
with high fuel prices encouraging a shift away from
combustion engine vehicles.
However, the risks aren’t all to one side. Demand for more
environmentally friendly new cars has picked up, and that
could push up prices for those vehicles. In addition, we’re
watching to see if high fuel costs are spilling over into
other prices as we’re seeing in sectors like construction.

Core inflation around 3%, not easing.
With big swings in fuel costs and concerns that this is
spilling over into a broader lift in inflation pressures,
the RBNZ will be paying close attention to the various
measures of core inflation (core inflation measures
smooth through quarter-to-quarter swings in individual
prices and instead track the underlying trend in inflation).
We expect that core inflation measures will point to
continued firmness in underlying price pressures, with

several closely watched measures likely to take a step
higher this quarter. In terms of specifics, we expect:
• CPI ex-fuel inflation: +3.0% yr (vs +2.9% previously)
• CPI ex-fuel and food: +3.2% yr (vs +2.9% previously)
• CPI ex-fuel, food and household energy: +2.9% yr (vs
+2.5% previously)

### Page 3

3
Westpac New Zealand Economics
We’ll also be watching the distribution of price
changes to see if there’s been a material increase in
the number of prices that are increasing. That could
be an early indication that businesses, even those
with a limited exposure to fuel costs, are starting to
factor in expectations of continued high inflation when
setting prices.
As we’ve noted before, the various measures of core
inflation were not low even before the recent fuel shock.

That means the RBNZ has limited headroom to absorb
upside inflation surprises.
Much of that firmness in core inflation has been due
to increases in domestically oriented non-tradables
prices. We estimate these prices rose by 1.3% over the
September quarter. That would see annual non-tradables
inflation rising to 3.6%, up from 3.4% last quarter.
Non-tradables inflation is a key focus for the RBNZ and
it’s been running at these above-average levels for around

a year now. That’s despite the softness in economic
growth and the labour market. In part, this firmness in
domestic inflation is due to continued large increases in
administered prices, like council rates.
On the imported front, lower fuel costs are expected to
see tradables prices falling by 0.2% over the quarter.
That would result in annual tradables inflation dropping
back to 3.9% from 4.9% previously. Excluding food and
fuel costs, we estimate that tradables prices were up

2.2% over the past year (vs 1.9% in the year to June). That
would be the highest level in three years with the fall in
the NZD boosting import costs.

forecasts
How do our forecasts compare to the
RBNZ’s assumptions?
The RBNZ’s last published forecast from their September

policy statement assumed that consumer prices would
rise by 0.8%qtr (3.9%yr). That’s slightly higher than we’re
expecting. Most of the difference is because the RBNZ is
expecting higher imported inflation.
A result in line with our forecast would give the RBNZ a
little more breathing room ahead of their upcoming policy
review on 28 October. We’re expecting they will leave the
OCR on hold at this meeting, while markets are pricing in
a roughly 66% chance of a 25bp hike.

However, even if the overall inflation result is close to
forecast, the underlying detail will be important. The
RBNZ will be watching closely for signs that high fuel
prices are spilling over into other prices, especially with
global oil prices taking another step higher recently. With
that in mind, the strength of the various core inflation
measures will be a key focus for the RBNZ.
Furthermore, while annual inflation is expected to
soften in the September quarter, this is likely to be just a

temporary reprieve. With a sharp rise in fuel costs over
the past few weeks, inflation is set to reaccelerate back
over 4% before the end of this year, and it’s set to remain
above the RBNZ’s forecast for most of next year.
September monthly prices.
Our forecast for a 0.7% rise in consumer prices in the
September quarter incorporates information from
Stats NZ’s latest monthly prices update. That update
did reveal some softer than expected food price

movements in September. However, that was balanced
against larger than expected rises in airfares and holiday
accommodation costs.

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research

### Page 2

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2. 2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%) . 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.4% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4,25% - 4,50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth slowed to 4.3% y/y in Q2 2026 vs. 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth 
remains characterized by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports and the fragility of sectors that rely 
on domestic demand  has even widened this year . Domestic demand has weakened significantly in recent months . Retail sales have barely 
increased, the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting 
economic growth are expected to increase in the second half of the year. CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

EUROZONE 
Eurozone growth is expected to slow to 1.1% in 2026 (from 1.3% in 2025), before strengthening to 1.5% in 2027, supported by higher defence 
and infrastructure spending as well as the global AI capex cycle. The economy has proven resilient to the energy shock, notab ly thanks to the 
increasingly tangible impact of G erman defence spending on the real economy. Elevated energy prices in the autumn (especially for natural 
gas), combined with resilient growth, are set to accelerate inflation to a peak around the turn of the year. Annual average i nflation is thus 
expected to reach  3.0% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), before easing to 2.8% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, we 
anticipate another 25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause . That said, 
stronger than expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

### Page 3

FRANCE 
GDP growth remained low in Q2 (0% after -0.2% in Q1) as a result of deteriorated agricultural and construction output, as well as higher 
inflation (2.4% in 2026 according to our forecast, after 0.9% in 2025) . In 2026, GDP growth should  reach 0.5% (after 0.9% in 2025), driven 
mainly by exports.

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would ease slightly to 1.2% in 2026 (1.3% in 2025), before edging back to 1.3% in 2027. The economy has so far weathered 
both the energy price shock and domestic political volatility. That said, growth is expected to decelerate in the second half  of the year, while 
inflation is set to remain stubbornly above target (averaging 3.2% in both 2026 and 2027), driven by higher energy prices and mounting second-
round effects. In response, we expect the BoE to deliver a single insurance hike in November 2026, bringing the Bank Rate to 4.00%. The BoE 
is then expected to resume easing in H2 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank Rate to 3.50% by end-year.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 1.0% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.2% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy  and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of +2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end -2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Monetary Authority of Singapore (MAS)

We expect another slight increase in the 
SGD NEER policy band slope at the MAS’ 
October review, with no change to the 
band’s width or centre. Import cost 
pressures and resilient growth favour a 
calibrated tightening, rather than a more 
forceful adjustment, in line with the 
global monetary policy stance.

Also due to be released alongside the 
MAS’ policy decision are the 3Q26 
advance GDP estimates, which we 
expect to show another quarter of strong 
growth at 5.4% yoy (1.4% qoq sa), 
compared with 5.9% yoy (1.4% qoq sa) in 
2Q26. Robust economic performance 
continued to be driven by artificial 
intelligence ( AI)-led trade activity, 
supporting the manufacturing and 
wholesale trade sectors, while strength 
in the financial sector underpinned the 
resilience of the modern services cluster. 
This came despite a moderation of the 
transport & storage sector, and 
weakness in consumer -facing food 
services and retail sectors.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Malaysia

We expect Malaysia’s advance GDP 
estimate for 3Q26 to register above -
trend growth of 5.0% yoy, albeit 
moderating from 6.0% yoy  in 2Q26. The 
overall expansion continued to be 
supported by ongoing strength in the 
export-oriented manufacturing sector, 
underpinned by strong  AI-related 
demand for electronics, and sustained 
domestic demand, backed by healthy 
investment and private consumption 
trends. However, contraction in mining 
and weak agricultural activity dragged 
overall growth.

Singapore

We expect firm Singapore non -oil 
domestic exports (NODX) growth of 28.0% 
yoy in September 2026, although 
moderating from August’s exceptional 
surge of 46.2%. Although favourable 
base effects largely faded in September, 
electronics shipments likely remained 
the primary growth driver, underpinned 
by robust AI -related demand amid 
sustained global investment in AI 
infrastructure, while non-electronics 
exports were uneven.

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 9, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

China

Exports growth is expected to remain 
robust at 25.7% y oy in September, 
supported by continued strength in 
electronics demand. Both the official and 
Caixin manufacturing PMIs rose above 
the 50 expansion threshold during the 
month, pointing to improving 
manufacturing activity. External trade 
indicators also stren gthened, with 
average daily deadweight tonnage 
handled at China's 20 major ports 
accelerating from -0.7% yoy in August to 
7.2% in September. Freight activity 
improved as well, as growth in 
international cargo flights picked up 
from -1.4% y oy to 3.6% over the same 
period.

On the price front, factory -gate and raw 
material prices rebounded amid the 
renewed escalation in geopolitical 
tensions. Main raw material purchasing 
price and producer price subPMI 
increased from 56.6 and 50.4 to 60.8 and 
54.0 during the period. However, 
downstream consumer price pressures 
are likely to remain relatively subdued 
under the pricing-band mechanism. As a 
result, CPI inflation is expected to edge 
up from 0.8% y oy in August to 1.0% in 
September. Nevertheless, soft domestic 
demand is expected to continue 
constraining the pace of consumer price 
increases.

India

September inflation is likely to gather 
momentum to 5.7% y/y, partly driven by 
base effects, from 4.8% the month 
before. Food segments continue to 
register gains, with pressures 
broadening to include perishables, 
edible oils, rice, pulses, sugar, etc., 
according to high-frequency data. Under 
non-food, retail pump prices have not 
been adjusted further, though other fuel 
products, such as non -subsidised LPG, 
diesel and ATF (still up by double digits 
y/y, though slower than in August), are 
up on the year. Core  inflation is also 
likely to tick up, aligning with the central 
bank's view that price risks are no longer 
benign. Pressures have built up due to 
the overhang of an uneven and sub -par 
monsoon, drought conditions in parts of 
the country, and elevated oil pr ices. In 
the face of higher headline prints, we 
expect the RBI to keep the door open for 
another rate hike in December 2026.

## Document 379: US Equity Views_ Over-earning, but not an earnings bubble

Source PDF: all report pdf/US Equity Views_ Over-earning, but not an earnings bubble.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 18
Extraction status: ok

### Page 2

semiconductor share prices have recently lagged the trajectory of near-term EPS
estimates. Similarly, the market trades at a P/E discount relative to what a macro model
would imply given the current level of corporate profitability. Our 12-month S&P 500
return forecast of +14% (to 8,700) reflects the view that earnings growth, rather than
expanding valuations, will remain the primary driver of the bull market.
17 September 2026   2
Goldman Sachs US Equity Views

### Page 3

The magnitude of S&P 500 “over-earning”

S&P 500 earnings grew by an extraordinary 51% year/year in Q2 2026 and have 
grown by 26% during the past four quarters. During the last 30 years, the recent pace 
of earnings growth has been exceeded only during the post-recession rebounds in 2010 
and 2021.

The recent strength of S&P 500 earnings growth has raised investor concerns that 
current earnings power is unsustainable and that the market is in an “earnings 
bubble.” S&P 500 earnings have recently outpaced both their historical trend and their 
historical relationship with US GDP growth. S&P 500 4-quarter EPS growth has averaged 
7% during the past 30 years, and the relationship between S&P 500 earnings and US 
economic growth in recent decades would have pointed to a similar rate of earnings 
growth during the last few quarters.

### Page 4

The degree to which the US equity market is currently “expensive” depends in large 
part on the sustainability of recent earnings strength. The exceptional recent 
strength of earnings has driven an unusual wedge between equity valuation multiples 
based on near-term earnings and valuations on trend earnings. Because equity prices 
have failed to keep pace with surging earnings, near-term valuations show no hint of a 
bubble: The S&P 500 12-month P/E ratio has declined from 23x a year ago to 19x today 
and now matches its 10-year average. However, even an “average” multiple may be 
expensive if current earnings are unsustainable. The cyclically-adjusted P/E based on 
trailing 10-year earnings has climbed to one of its highest levels on record, falling shy of 
the highs of 1999-2000 but surpassing the level reached in 2021. Similarly, our 
colleagues have argued that the increase in market value of AI companies during the last 
few years requires an optimistic estimate of the future magnitude of AI-related profits 
and who will capture them.

### Page 5

The AI boom and S&P 500 earnings growth

Affirming investor concerns that US companies are “over-earning,” there are three 
factors that are boosting earnings today but will contribute to deceleration going 
forward: 1) AI capex spending, 2) semiconductor margin expansion, and 3) earnings 
from private investment gains.

AI capex 
The AI investment boom is driving nearly half of S&P 500 EPS growth this year, but 
its contribution will fade going forward. The mega-cap US hyperscalers are on track to 
spend $800 billion on capex this year, an increase of 94% vs. 2025. That spending is 
flowing through to the earnings of the AI infrastructure complex including 
semiconductor, tech hardware, industrials, and utilities companies, which are collectively 
accounting for roughly half of consensus S&P 500 earnings growth this year. The AI 
boom is also having a secondary impact on earnings outside of the infrastructure 
complex, for example by boosting capital markets activity and consumer wealth. Both 
consensus and GS analyst forecasts show hyperscaler capex growing at a slower rate in 
coming years, which should result in decelerating earnings growth for much of the AI 
infrastructure complex and a fading tailwind to S&P 500 earnings.

### Page 6

Hyperscaler depreciation expenses will continue to increase as capex growth 
decelerates, further dampening the boost of AI investment spending to S&P 500 
earnings growth. We estimate a drag from hyperscaler depreciation expenses on S&P 
500 earnings growth of 5pp in 2027, offsetting nearly half of the 11pp boost to earnings 
from capex spending. By 2028, the drag from depreciation should offset the S&P 500 
earnings uplift from continued capex spending.

### Page 7

If capex were fully expensed, consensus 2027 S&P 500 EPS would be 22% lower than 
under current accounting rules. The current gap between actual reported and 
hypothetical fully-expensed earnings is one of the widest in recent decades, exceeded 
only as earnings fell more quickly than capex during the recessions of 2001 and 2008.

Hyperscaler capex has consistently surprised relative to consensus estimates during 
the last few years, and the potential for additional surprises going forward creates a 
wide range of potential S&P 500 earnings outcomes. Our equity analysts expect 
hyperscaler capex will total $1.2 trillion in 2027 and $1.4 trillion in 2028, in which case 
the current tailwind from AI capex to S&P 500 EPS will fade by 2028. Exhibit 10 below 
outlines upside and downside scenarios around that base case. Next year, a $250 billion 
surprise in hyperscaler capex — in either direction — would shift S&P 500 EPS growth by 
about 6 pp in the same direction.  
We estimate that hyperscaler capex would need to decline by roughly 30%, to $570 
billion, to offset earnings growth for the rest of the S&P 500 in 2027. This decline 
would reduce earnings for the AI infrastructure complex by about 40%, leaving their 
profits about 25% above 2025 levels.

### Page 8

Semiconductor margin expansion 
A sharp increase in profit margins for many semiconductor companies has 
contributed to the above-trend recent pace of S&P 500 earnings growth. The 
combination of strong demand and limited supply has boosted semiconductor prices 
and gross margins, with the most pronounced margin expansion among memory stocks. 
S&P 500 memory firms currently enjoy gross margins of roughly 80%, more than double 
their historical average. Across the semiconductor industry, gross margins sit at their 
highest level in decades, and consensus expects margins to remain at similar levels 
through 2028.

Margin expansion has accounted for a large share of recent semiconductor earnings 
growth, but that boost should fade going forward. We estimate that about 1/4 of S&P 
500 Semiconductor earnings growth in 2026 is being driven by gross margin expansion. 
Our equity analysts believe the balance of memory supply and demand is likely to 
remain tight through 2027, with incremental supply additions unlikely to impact industry 
margins until 2028 at the earliest. However, they expect the rate of incremental margin 
expansion to slow meaningfully next year, reducing the contribution of semiconductors

S&P 500 earnings growth in the next few years will be highly sensitive to the

investment, increasing supply, and/or technological shift lowers semiconductor prices 
and profit margins, S&P 500 EPS growth would also disappoint. We estimate that each 
percentage point change in S&P 500 semiconductor gross margins next year would shift 
S&P 500 EPS growth by about 1 pp.

### Page 10

“Other income” from private investment gains 
Appreciating equity investment stakes are also temporarily inflating S&P 500 
earnings. Mega-cap tech earnings have recently been boosted by large GAAP “other 
income” related to their equity investments. In Q2 2026, the mega-cap tech companies 
enjoyed unrealized investment gains in private companies that totaled roughly $150 
billion, translating into 12% of S&P 500 EPS. We expect H2 2026 reports will include 
additional “other income” that should be larger than the gains in Q1 but smaller than Q2 
totals. We expect a much smaller contribution in 2027. The complete removal of this 
“other income” next year would create a drag of 8 pp on S&P 500 earnings growth in 
2027 relative to 2026, all else equal.
